6.0 ACTION IN THE TACTICAL ENVIRONMENT

In a tactical situation, action is resolved in terms of feet and
seconds, During the MERP tactical sequence, cach character may
take only one action once every 1) seconds: he may move, altack,
prepare a spell, cast a spell, make a moving mancuver, make a static
maneuver, oF perform some other action, This 10 second period is
called a “round”. A rownd usually takes considerably longer than 10
seconds of real time to resolve.

Certain factors may affect the action that a character may lake,
Most of these are obvious and can be resolved by using common
sense (2.2... a character with a broken anm can use a two-handed
WEEP Of a bee) an wnconschus character can perform mo action,
except perhaps breathing; etc.). The Gamemacter & of course the
Final authority on these matters,

Certain spells allow more than one action in a round (e.g,, Haste
allows 2 actions to be performed im one round). The use of spells to
move (e.g., Levitation, Flying, Windwalking, ete.) count as the sole
acon for a round unless they are merely used to hold position (i.<.,
hemverh,

Cenain eritical resulis from attacks limit actions, while other
criticals restrict activity (e.g., 20 to aciivity), Unless stated other-
wise in the critical deseription, these subtractions from activity
apoly to all bonuses except the Defensive Bonu: and Resistance Roll
bonuses.

The Gamemaster and the players should divide up the work in-
volved in controlling a tactical combat situation. The Gamemaster
has to handle the physical layout and all of the non-player
characters, but the players can help with other factors. One player
should keep track of Ube damage taken and status of each character
(asbect is provided in MEAP for this and may be phiio-copied for
hon hnmercial purposes), Another player should keep track of the
round and action sequencing (soa Section 6.2). Amother player can
record activity by player characters which can later lead to ex-

perience points, Other players can handle the tables and read off

results during play. By dividing up these tasks the game flows very
smoothly, everyone gets involved, and ma one is swamped with
work. '

The MERP system for tactical action and combat is simplified
and abstracted lo increase is playability. [f beginning fantasy role
players have mastered MEAP and desire more detailed and realiatic
coma, LClE."s Aino Lew at Claw Low provides an expanded
combat system, with individual attack tables for the various
weapond, 2 classifications of armor, more detailed critical strike
tables. and rules that allow moee flexibility.

6.1 REPRESENTING THE PHYSICAL SITUATION

The Gumcraster must decide how to keep track of movement,
the relative positions of all of the characters, ranges for firing
missiles and casting spells, and the layout of the area in which they
are adventuring. This asually require: a playing surface that
represents the physical situation and playing pitees representing the
characters and other combatants, Many possibilities exist for such
Play aids, and the Gamemaster may elect to ose whichever seems
Most appeopriale for his game and fora particular sitaation. Below
we present afew of the mest helpful display techniqwes and hints,

The Giaeremaster can skebch ther layout on. sheet of paper tor on
a black board) and mark the position of each character on it. For
emiall sheets of paper we suggest a scale of 1" = $0". For a larger sue-
face, 1° = ori” = 10 would be more appropriate. You can keep
track of distance with a ruler, The problem with this technique ts
lack of detail and the hassles of erasing each time a character moves.

To solve part of this problem we suggest that a playing piece be us.
ed to represent the position of cach character. These can be as
tlaborate as the players and Gamemaster wish. Suggestions inclade:
comomercially available miniatures (small statues of varlous types of
Taniasy inhabitans and creatures), chess pieors, coins, or 4" square
1 1" spuare pieces of cardboard marked with the characters names,
The cardboard pieces are particularly easy bo make and use; they can
be colored or decorated to aid the favor of play.

We have inclided in WER two sheets of 8 "2 11" poper (hex
paper) with different sized grids conasting of hexagons placed on
them, These sheets make keeping track of distances easier: just
count the number of hexagons between two points, Lf playing pieces
are weed cach playing piece can Occupy a separate hexagon bo in-
dicate tls location.

Most Linge phota-capying centers have a transparency machine
which is capable of copying any black and white figure ante a clear
Plastic sheet, We suggest thal you lake a piece of the hexagon paper
inchuded in MEAP and have a transparcecy made. This can be laid
over any sort of Layout, superimposing a grid an it.

6.2 THE SEQUENCE OF ACTIONS
Actions are normally performed in the following order daring a
round (unless a character cancels his action or puts it inte an “oppar-
tunity state}:
Ll} Prepare or caat a spell.
2) Missile and thrown weapon attacks, missile parrying.
and loading, reloading.
3] Moving Mare rere.
4) Melee attacks and ovelee parrying.
3) Slovene.
4) Statle maneuvers.
= Onientation rolls are used when required by circumstance.
= Conflicting actions may modifiy this sequence (see Section 6.7).
At the beginning of each round, each player should state or write
doen (the Gamemaster must decide) what action he wishes 10 per-
form for that rownd. Al the same time, the Gamemaster should
decide what actions the non-player characters will take. The actions
are then resolved in the order specified by the sequence above, The
following sections describe how to resolve the various actions, The

sumple adventure presented in Section 1.2 is a good example of ac-
tions taken on a round by round basis.

6.3 PREPARING AND CASTING SPELLS

The cumber of rounds that a character (the caster) spends prepar-
ing o spell affects the effectiveness of a spell by modifying the attack
roll when the spell is cast. The modificstions are:

-30 for ne preparation rounds
-12 for one preparation round
+0 for two preparation rounds
+10 for three preparation rownds
+20 for four preparation rounds

All preparation rounds for a given spell must immediately follow
one another and immediately precede the casting round (unbess
cadting the spell is put into an opportunity state; see Section 6.2).
During a preparation round, a character may abo move wp to 10 feet
when movement 6 resolved. In the sample adventure im Part [ {pe},
Agonar prepared hie spell during the round that the Ore moved
towards Chinta Kari.

Lt requires ane rowned’s action to actually cast a spell and have it
take effect. The target must be in sight or detected in some manner.
Ter cast a spell an “attack” roll most be made (such as Agemar's
Levitate spell in Part {pits sample adventure). All spells mons make an
“attack” poll ewen if the spell doesn’t actually attack a target (there is a
Chince of failure for every spell), Bolt spell attacks are resolved on Table
AT-7, Ball spell atiacks are resolved on Table AT-8, and alll other spells
are resolved on Table AT-9, Quring a casting round, a charadter muy
ako move up to 20 feet when movement is nesolved, In the sample
miventure in Part! (po), Agonar cast his Sleep spell the round that ihe
Orc atiem pied bo attack Chinta Kawi.

6.4 MOVEMENT

Movement is simultaneous for all characters. [f there are conflicts
fopponents have both specified movement), players should wrive
down where they intend to move. The Gamemiaster shevuhd wee ccem-
mon sense bo interpret the intent of each character, and characters
should be able to modify their actual movement during play in order
10 Temel bo circumstances. Bach character may move up to 30" plus
his meovernent and mancuver bonus without a maneuver roll, or up
to dowhbe that (running) if he successfully makes a maneuver roll
(ane next section).

A character may shift items and equipment on his person (e.g...
draw a weapon, sling a shield, take out some herbs, etc.}), but the
distance that be may move i: decreased by 10 feet for each item
shifted (for that round). Terrain may also slow a character: going up
shill, through a thicket, across a stream, across sand, etc, [t is sug
gested that a Gamesnaster cut movement by half for such terrain.

lia character wishes lo move double his normal movement (run,

ig 10, the character may move up to double his normal movement
rate. For every increment of 10 that the number ia under 100 the
doubled movement rate is decreased by 1. For every increment of
10 that the mumber is over 1) the doubled movement rate is in-
creaaed bry LO.

The number of rounds that a character is capable of running is
equal te his Constitution bones plus 3, For cach round that be rests
he will be able to regain 2 rounds of running capability. For ex-
ample, a character with a Constitution bonus of + 1) could ran 14
rownods before having to rest; orhe could run for 0 rounds reducing
his running capabllin’ to 5, rest for 2rends and ged 4 rounds of run
ning capability back for a total of 9, and then run for 9 more rounds
before being forood bo reat.

6.5 MANEUVERS

Maneuvers are actions (other than atiacks and spell casing)
which under normal circumstances haw a chance of failing and
usually entail an element of risk, Thus nommal movement and ac-
tivities (e.g-, walking, climbing stair, drawing a weapon, €c.) are
not maneuvers, while unusual activities (swimming, climbing a
rope, Cunning up or down stairs, opening a locked chest, etc.) and
activities performed under stress (running, dodging, stalking,
hiding, etc.) are maneuvers and require rolls, The Gamensaster is the
final judge as te what isa maneuver and requires a roll, and what ls
nommal activity and deen't cequine a poll, Im AP, maneuvers are
classified as Moving Maneuvers or os Static Maneuvers. Each class
of maneuvers is resolved differently,

6.51 MOVING MANEUVERS

and any complex or unusual movement (acrobatics, dodging,
tumbling, diving, etc.) If a character decides to make a Moving
Maneuver as his action for a turn, the Gartemaster should assign it a
difficulty: routine, casy, light, odie, bard, very hard, extremely
hard, sheer folly, or absurd. The manewver must still be within the
physioal expabilities of the character, and is limited to covering @
digtance no more than ballf of the character's normal (not runing)
movement. The Gamemaster may also assign an additional
modification to the maneuver roll (e.g., an unassisted runcing leap
scross a 40? chasm for a herman might be given a difficulty of “ab-
curd -L0" since the werkd record is only around 28°). Assigning dif-
ficulty requires a farniliarity with the Moving Maneuver Talble MIT-1
and a subjective decion by the Gaumemiasier (practice ane cx-
penne will help).

After the difficulty is assigoed, the character may them decide 1
to attempt the maneuver (ie., be reconsiders and decides oot to do
it}. In this case, be cancels his action and may perform some other
limited action as described in Section 6.2.

6.52 STATIC MANEUVERS

Static Maneuvers include tracking, hiding, picking locks, disatm-
ing traps, reading runes, using items, perception, inthuencing other
characters, and any other comples of unusual activity noc involving
significant movement, If a character chooses a Static Mancuver as
hz action for a round, the Gamenaster must assign a difficulty and
then the character must decide whether or oot to actually perform
the maneuver (see Section 6.2 for the effects of canceling an action).
The Gamemaster may require more than one round for maneuvers
which he decides are very difficult or complex. He may also allow a
character lo decrease the difficulty of appropriate maneuvers by us-
ing, multiple rounds.

Ifthe character decides. to proceed with the maneuver, he makes
an open-ended roll and adds his appropriate bonus along with any
other applicable modifications. The resulting total is creas-indexed
with the appropriate column on the Static Maneuver Table MT-2 to
obtain the oianewver result, Te summarize:

TMS LYeT.

roll is mabe,

6.6 ATTACKS

This section describes attacks and the process used to resolve
them. A very detailed example combat is included to Hustrate enany
of the rales outlined in this section.

Attacks occur when a character (ihe attacker) attempls to affect
or harm an opponent (ihe defender or target). The attecks fall inte
three categories: spell attacks, minile {includes thrown weapons) at-
tacks, and melee (weapon and animal) attacks. Bach type of attack
is normally resolved during a different part of the round, I a
character’s action for a round is an altack (spell, missile, or melee,
he may move opto 10 during the proper part of the owned (i,¢,, dur:
ing movernem). Simple actions such as dropping whatever is in hus
hands may be performed with the permission of the Gamernaster,
who may assign a penalty (to the character's OB) if mecessary.

lia charactersaction ie melee, he has the option cel movie he Lo
just before melee attacks are resolved (i.c., Ure movement is a.charge
and thua pant of the atteck). The attacker may also shift an item
(e.g. draw a weapon, unsling ashield, take herbs from abel pouch,
etc), but then his Offensive Homus modifbed by -3,

An attack is resolved by the attacker making an open-ended al-
tack roll, adding any applicable modifications, and applying the
total to the appropriate attack table. The result will usually require a
second roll on the Fumble and Failure Table, a critical table, or the
Resistance Roll Table, Applicable modifications are given oc che at-
tack tables, and special atiack properties are given in the Statistics
tables: the Weapon Statistics Table CST-1, the Animal Statistics
Table CST-Z, amd the Spell Sintistics Table CST-3, Toe sumemargize:

L) Attacker makes an Open-ended attack roll

2) Uf am UM (unmodified) result is oitained proceed to 4.
3) All applicable modifications are made to the roll.

4) The total is applied te the appropriate attack table,

§) The result may require a roll ona second table.

6.61 SEQUENCING ATTACKS OF THE SAME TYPE

All spell attacks are resolved simultaneously, All missile and
throan attacks are resolved simultaneously, Melee attacks are
resolved one at atime; the character with the highest Movement anc
Maneuver Bonus resolves his attack first, then the character with the
second highest procemds, etc. If the Kioverment and Maneuver
bonuses of several melecing characters are equal, those attacks are
resolved simultaneously.

Ifa character's attack i an opportunity action (delayed from a
previous round, or from earlier in the current round), it is always
resolved before other attacks of the the same typ.

6.62 TARGET CHOICE AND PARRYTING

Just before melee attacks are resolved all combatants must decide
which opponents they will attack and how much of thelr Offensive
Bonuses they wish to perry with. A character may only attack a foe
whe is adjacent to hin (ichin 10%, This is an accoplable form of
limited movement. [f more than one foe is available, ihe character
must choose one oo attack. He may attack one foe and ose his shield
bonus against another (see Section 2.72), if it is physically possible.

Mole Parying — A character may “parry” the melee attack of the
larget be is attacking in melee. He may reduce his Offensive Bonus
by any ammount (but mot to bess than 0), and his target's melee attack
roll is then modified downward by that amount. In effect, the
character may use all or part of his Offensive Bonus to subtract frorn
ihe melee attack roll of ame of his adjacent foes. The character «till
makes a melee attack roll agains the foe that he is parrying, adding
whatever part of his Offensive Bonus thal was not used bo parry (iL

A stunned character ora character using a 2-Handed weapon may

When the characters indicate their actions for a round (see Sec-

with,

Missile Parrying — (f acharacter has a shield and he is facing an op-

6.64 MATIMUM AND MINIMUM RESULT LIMITATIONS

The attack tables have maxicnum and minimum results, If a
modified attack roll exceeds a maximum result, the roll is treated as
the highest non-UM revuli allowed. If a modified attack roll is less
than the minimum result, the roll is treated as the lowest non-LM
result.

On most of the atiack tables the maximum result is 130 or one less
han an unmodified result, while the mainimum result is usually one
more than the unmodified fumble failure result, Qn the Bolt Attack
Table AT-7 the maxitoum result is determined by the spell type; an
ihe animal aviack tables (Table AT-§ and Table AT=§) the maximum
result is determined by the animal's size (small, mectium, large or
huogeb.

6.65 ATTACK RESULTS

An atiack may have several results depending upon the attack roll
(modified or unmodified). These results include hits, fumbles,
Failures, criticals, and Resistance Roll modifications.

FUMBLES AND FAILURES — If an attack roll results in on “F*
result, the attack has foiled ana oa failore roll must be made on the
Fumble and Failure Tables FT-1, FT-2, FT-3, of FT-4. A
PumbleFailare roll is modified only by the type of weapon o7 spell
used to make the attack, Uf the atteck is mace with a weapon, a Fum-
ble occurs if the original unmadified reli is within the fumble range
of ihe weapon being used (see Table CST-| for the varus ranges).
Animal attacks which fail merely result in no damage being given.

HITS — The number resolts on all of the attack tables except the
Base Attack Table AT-9 equal the number of hits delivered to the
iairget.

CRITICALS — The letter results (A, B,C, D, and E) indicate that
the tanget has been given one or more critical strikes (critical or crit).
The type and number of criticals depends upon the type of the
weapon or spell used. This information is given in the statistic tables
{Tables CST-1, CST-2, and CST-3). To determine the result of the
erliical 4 second roll is made, modified by a bonus based upon the
severity or the critical (ive., what lenter), and then applied 10 the ap-
Mopriate critical column. Finally, the resolts of the critical are ap-
flied to the target.

If the target takes a critical reault that lasts for a certain number of
rounds and he has not yet performed hia action for the current
round, the current round counts as oneof the rounds affected by the
result.

The criticals give very specific damage to targets that receive
them. All of the damage specifeed Gy cricals should be sell-
explanatory except for the following very common results:

+ # hits, — This many hits (#) are taken in addition to the
normal hit result derived from the attack table used (e.g.
“443 hits.)

# hits per roond, — Each rownd after the ert is taken, the
targct takes this many (#) additional hits due te pain and
bleeding (¢.g., “3 hita‘round.").

Stunned # rounds. — The target is stunned for this many (#)
rounds, during which he may not attack and may only parry
with 50% of his Offensive bonus. Any other action requires
a maneuver roll modified by -S0(e.¢., “Stunmed § rownds.").

-# to activity, — All of the target's bonuses (except DB and
RR's) are lowered by the # (e.g., “10 nn activity.”),

are uniformly bad.

7.0 SPELL LISTS, TABLES, AND RECORD SHEETS

The Middle-earth Role Playing system contains. a number of lists,
tables and sheets which are used to play ihe game and te an the
Gamemaster and players. The spell lists contain the descriptions and
Parameters of apells that certain characters may cast. The tables
range from the tactical combat resolution tables to the strategic
tables for weather, treasure and prices. The record sheets provide
mechanisms for keeping track of infonmavion required for playing
ihe game,

7.1 SPELL LISTS

Fach spell list is organized with the apell list tithe at the top,
followed by asummary of the spells on that list, and concluding with
the detailed deseviptions and explanations of each spell. The
descriplions of the individual spells are ordered according to the
levels of the spells (kevels | through 10), Each spell’s area of effect,
duration, and range are listed in the sammarics. A key to some of the
abbreviations and notion used bio the spell summarics and
descriplions is provided below,

that the physical or mental condition created by the spellis real. The
“Permanent” comdition created may be disturbed by another spell of
natural physical forces. Forexample, Freeze Water will create a “per -

SPELL PARAMETERS:

Area of Effect: This is the scope of the spell's effects, Depending
dpon the spell. this can be the size and shape of the aren affected, the
type of target, the number of targets, the moss affected, ete. For
aime spells the area of effect varies with the caster’s level (e.g..
10' Rly] means a radios equal to ten feet per evel of the caster). The
artaor umber givenis an upper limit amd can be reduced if the caser
wishes.

Drarathon: The duration of a spell determines how long the elects of
the spell last. The duration can be only for an instant (—}, for a fied
amountof cline (e.g... 1 min), for an amcrant of time dependent upon
the caater’s level fe.g., 1 min-Ivt), for os bong os the caster comeon-
rakes (2), or indefinite (P or varies). The duration given is an upper
limit, and the caster may cancel ome of his spells (befoce its duration
has passed) by concentrating forone round, Canceling a spell will net
always negate its effects (¢.g., canceling a Wall of Fire does not do
away with any burns which it may have caused),

Range: The range of a spell is the distance that may separate the
caster from the target of his spell or the direct cifects of bis spell,
Typical raspes ane: only on the caster himself (self), touching the
tarpet (touch), a fined distance (e.g., LOM), or a distance dependent
upos the caster’s level (e.g., LO lel). The range given an upper limit
and spclls may be cast ata target (or take effect) at lesser ranges. Ifa
spell bas an area effect, the center for the approximare cemter) of he
arcaof effect mua be within range, but part of the area effected may

SPELL CLASSES:

{E) = Elemental spell: These spells use the foree of the spell to
manipulate physical elemeats (heat, cold, wind, light, water, earth,
sound, smell, laste, Louch). Thest cements (and mot the spell) are
ued ie either directly attack a lange of to affect Une senses of a Large,
Since the clemecnts arc real, no Resistance Rollsare normally allowed,
Elemental attack spells and illusion apells fall iano this class.

(F) — Force spell: These spells involve the direct manipulation of
muatier, energy, the elements, or living being through the uae of a
spell's force. [f the spell has a target capable of resisting, it must make
a Resistance Roll to see if itis affected by the spell. Most base antack
spells Fall inte: this class.

(P) — Passive spell: These spells wsually only indirectly or passively
affect a target. Thus if a Resistance Rall (Ciamemaster’s decision) is
allowed, its purpose is only to determine if the target & aware of the
spell, Many detection spells ancl protection spells fall inte this class.

(U) — Utility spell: These spells only affect the caster, a willing
target, or a target incapable of resistance. Thus, Reslarance Rolls are
isually not necessary. A willing tanget who if capable of resisting
may stil be required to make a Resistance Roll, bur itis modified by
0, Most healing spells fall into this class.

(1) = Informational spell: These spell involve gathering information
through means that de net require ay Resistance Rolb.

DEFINITIONS OF SPELL TERMS:

Animal — A living creature of feeling and voluntary motion, but
not particularly intelligent.

Being — Anvintelligent creature, incluching all humanoid types, en-
chanted creatures, etc,

Concentration — If a caster “concentraies", he may not perform
any action other than moving upto Ml each round. Lf he is attacked
and given hits, he must make an onentaion roll to maintain con-
centration, Concentration is automatically broken if the character
is stunned or rendered uncomscipus. Certain spells (with a duration
other ¢han SO") allow the caster to break concentration and then
return te comcentration later fe.g., mast detect spells).

Inotpanic = Mot of or deriving from living organisms (¢.@., iron,
copper, silver, water, glass, etc.).

Organ = A differentiated pan of an arganism, adapted for a
specific function fe.g-, a liver isan organ, a heart is an organ, the
muscles in a leg are net an organi.

Organic = OF or deriving from living organisms (e.g., leather,
bone, coucn, wool, €ic,),

7.3 GENERAL TABLES AND RECORD SHEETS

The general tables serve a variety of purpoars during character
generation (Section 3) and character development (Sections 2 amd 3h.
The reat of the general tables are used by che Gamemaster to create,
control and resolve situations in the strategic cavironment. The
record sheets are meant to be used as play akls, and may be photo
cepted for non-commercial purposes. Some of the general tables are
included in this section; others are included in the sections in which
their ose 1% described. The Contents’ section at the beginning of
MERF gives page surnisers for the bocalions Of the various tables.

ST-2 — CREATURE SUMMARY TABLE

Mo world is campleie without the inclusion of the Fauna and in-
digenous musties of cach locale, Por the sake of convenience and
case of explanation, the “normal” Fauna of Middle-earth is kept
separate from the spectacular ond deadly animals and monsters
that comprise such a large portion of the atmosphere of any
Tolkien-based game.

The combat capabilities of the animals most likely to be en-
countered adventuring in Middle-earth are sammarized in the first
part of Table ST-2. All of these creatures should be familiar to the
players and thus are not fully descobed. Names are generic, such as
“small cat" or “large dog.” Anytime an animal is encountered the
nearest compatible se.ofstachstics should be axed. Thud, acharging
tiger would use the “large cat” row of statistics, while a water buf-
fale would use the “ball” pow. One thimg to nemeni ber 16 that most
animals willeot atiack men voluntarily. Encounters with an animeal
should have a reason sock as the animal being summoned or highs
ing to protect its young. In general, squadrons of moose do no
roam the woods hooking for a) group of player chareclers lo mug.

8.0 APPENDIX 1 — RACE DESCRIPTIONS

The following are general descriptions of the predominant races
of Middle-earth. Naturally, certain individuals will differ to varying
degrees. Certain modifiers are included for purposes of realism, but
GMa shevald feel free to conit them if they consider them to be un-
wieldly.

DWARVES (rr, Ahazad, sing. Afasad)

The Dwarves are deacendants of the Seven Fathers, the original
lords crafted from the earth by the Yala Aue, He isthe Smith of the

Most groups favor the rocky highlands and deep caverns of the
mountains, for the Dwarves, perhaps more than any race, re-
member their origin and heritage.

ELY¥ES (Quendi

Elves were the first to awaken and venture onto Middle-earth.
They aré a graceful and nevble race of immortal beings who resemble
Men, but shine wilh an inner ghew which betrays the spirit of unique
thoughts and gifts, Mo race has been blessed with more, or cursed so
much by the hand of Fate.

Though basically similar to mortal men in most ways, Elwes have
several important, if subdle differences. As a race they are taller than
humans but they are uniformly slender and have less body hale,
Flven men have no beards. Fine features and fair, unmarred akin
combine with sparkling eyes ta give the Elf an enchanted aura. A
strange deftness and case of movement give them a graceful quality
and it is not surprising that they are very agile and 40 light afoot;
they leave few traces of their passing and can walk on the surface of
freshly fallen smow. Their gentle physical appearance belies great
atrengihs, however, for they are highly resistant to the crippling ef-
fects of exireme temperatures, and are immune io disease and ill-
Teed.

They are algo immortal, and age in a graceful, nearly impercep-
ble manner. Elves commonly die from only two causes: through
vidkence, or asa result of a weariness of ihe world. In the latter case
an Elfis simply overcome with grief over the course of time, thereby
losing the wall to live. All the Elves who poss away are gathered in the
Halls af Mandas, the Piece of Awaiting, in the far west of Aman
(¥alingr). There they await the End of the Workl, of are released
back inte the workd to replace apother of thelr line who has per-
ished. In a sense, Elves are often reborn as descendants of
themselves.

The Elves revere the Valar ancl many know ther nature well. Still,
they have no formal religion: instead they show their respect
through poriry and song and gather to celebrate life and the gilts
from on high. This respect for dhe way of things is Largely tied to
their acceptance of their close tes to the Fate set down in the Song of
Creation, akthough much of it alko stems from an understanding
and joy regarding the creations of mature which have been banded
down an overscen by the Valar, Above all but Ero they worship
Farda, Ceeeen of the Valor and fairest of all creations. She is the
bringer of light and they call her “Lady of the Stars" (Efendi ar
Eliereth).

Elves do not need sleep. Instead, they receive reat through medita-
tion invelving memories, past events they recall with remarkable
vividness. Normally they go inte this trance-like state for approx:
mmely two hours each day, although they can funcieen for many
days with litt or no relief. While in their meditative state, Elves are
extremely difficult te awaken; they rise at a point previously de-
cided, This monnmer of rest is in keeping with the Elven fondness for
the night. Men have often referred to them as people of the staré
with good reason, for Elves see as well on a slar-lt evening 25a man
would at the height of day. Elven sight is ideal for the partial
darkmess of the shadowy forest or cloudy sky, and allows them 4
mobility unlike that of any other race. In absolute darkness,
however, they suffer as others do; they can nem see at al

Their sense of hearing & also superb, and no dowbt acoounis for
the Elven skills with mesic. Their reverence for song is unparalleled
and hes affected their language and way of keeping precious
records, The Elves were first to use spoken words and have taught
the ether races of the gift of speech; thus their own mame for their
kind: “Quen? — the “Speakers". All of their speech has a musical
quality when spoken properly, lending itself well to verse. Elven
Hards, then, have had litth trouble in maintaining the histories and
epics of thelr race as a collection of wondrous songs and spoken
pereiry.

There are two major groupings of Elves in Middle-earth. Their
split in the early Firat Age formed the basis for the development of
two separate Elven cultures. The most exalted group is the Eldar,
the three kindreds (Molder, Foaver, and Telert) which made the
Great Journey across Middle-earth during the Firat Age. Most
settled fora time in Amman (the Undying Lands), of along the shores
of now-ssunken Beleriand. Only the Noldor and Sindar (a group of
Teleri} remained in Middle-earth following the Har of Brava ihad
ended the First Age. All other folk are called Awan or Silvan Elves,
They are commonly called Woeod-efves.

From these cultures come the three groupe which form the Elven
races oF Middle-earth: (1) the Noldor, (2) the Sencar, amd (3) the
Silvan Elwes.

MEN ‘Hildor ar Atari

WMen were the Secondborn and first arose in the Far East during
the First Age. There, ina land called Afdddrien, they awoke to find a
wild continent and it was sometime before they firet encountered the
Firstborn, or Elves. It was the Elves who gave them the name Aldor
(Et, “Followers”), alihough they are now more commonly called by

Men differ from the Elves in many ways, the least subtle of which

a fire and pacsion unlike any other race. They are also varied and
aften flawed and vulnerable, Unlike the Firatborn, they are quite
susceptible to disease and temperature extremes. At the same time,
however, they rarely weary of life,

There are two greater groupings of Men: the High Men, who are
commonly called Edain; and the Common Men, thos who did mot

speak of the Mortharen as a third group, MWidale Men who are ra-
cally clase to the Edain, but did not benefit from any contact or
alliance with the Elwes.

High Men are exceptionally large, averaging 6"3" to 4'T" and
weighing 200 te 27% pounds. Their great bones and musculature
gives them a grand appearance; only the Moldor Elves rival their
strength. They are oot as almble or resilient as the Elves, however.
The hair of High Men is dark-brown or coal-black — their eyes blue,
grey, or black — and they tend toward fair skin, Those of pure
blood live as lomg as 250-300 years.

Common Men are more numerous and less blessed. Stocky anal
relatively short (5° 10 64"), they have produced a wide variety of
types. Their hair and skin colors vary from the deepest brown and
blacks to the lightest shades of Mond and cream. Unlike the High
Men, many wear beards and mustaches. From their ranks come the
vast majority of Middle-earth's mannish lines.

WABGS — As the Great Eagles are greater than earth cagles, so are

WEREWOLVES = Evil spirits held in thrall by a greater Evil, the
werewolves were the special servants of Sauron during the Wars of
Beleriand in the First Age. Werewolves speak both Black Speech and
most Elvish tongues, and this is but a small part of their cleverness.
Like Balrops, they force opponents to resist their terrible presence,
although the RR is based only ona Sth level attack, Lesser Maiar or
ancher type of spirit, the Werewolves are not slain easily. If they are
slain, the spirit is only temporarily removed from the physical plain
and. will return to seek out its slayer.

WIGHTS — Wights appear as dark, shadowy human forms with
eyesakin co faint lights. They inhabit the same world as the dreaded
NaxeGl and arc hard to perceive a5 anything other than dark, misty
shapes. If seen with the aid of special magic, Wight will take the tat-
tered forme of great lords of men with cold cruel eyes. Wights inhabit
the old graves of Cuinedain, sent as messengers of despair by Sauron
and the Witch-king of Angmar.

Wights are extremely hard to kill, They take half damage from
non-muagic weapons, roll on the Large Critical Table, and have the
same weapon dissolution effect of Maxgil at third level effect.
Anyone touched by the Wight, or hit by its weapon, is affected like
the Margdl’s Black Breath at a 7th level effect. Unlike the Black
Breath, this effect is immediate, Wights also affect onlookers asa
Balrog, but at only the 3th bevel,

The only way to permanently killa Wight is to kill it physically,
open the grave it inbalbots, and spread the graves contents out for alll
pastershy to take and thus scatter. If this is not dene, the grawe will
quickly be reinhabited by another similar Wight. Three different
types of Wights are given in the chart. The power of a given Wight
should relate directly to the power of the lord buried in ihe grave the
Wight inhabits.

THE RUINED CASTLE

Three days north of the Last Inn amd just off a tobutary of the
Klitheithel stand the crumbling, eemie ruins of a once-magnificent
extk, Hervber Gilar, the Dewelling of the Lord of High Sorcery.
The stream that once fed a moat shielding the stronghold froc
frontal attack has now cut ihrough the walland eaten away al Tower
9. which is flooded, and has tumbled Tower 4, which has dammed the
stream and diverted its flow. (A subterranean river skirts dhe walls it
the rear and poovides escape from the deepest bowels of the keep, if
ome makes it that far in his exploration of the much-feared Castle in
the Woods.) The main gateis blackened and blasted, The entrance on

and peripatetic Mage and agent of the Witch-king known to the Ores

fandom evil-doer from his regular appearances at the Inn, where
he sings and charms audience as Turin, the wandering minstrel.)
Ar-Gilar is, in actuality, @ fallen Edain noble, the basiard son of a
power!) bord of Rhudaur, who was denied any Inberhance but
received an extraordinary education and developed a geet for
deception, the use of magic and sorcery, and revenge upon the Edain
nobility. Thus was he led by fate to the dark gates ofthe Witch-king's
realm.

Ar-Gillar's duties are threefold: first, to gather all the information
he can about the region's inhabitants and their movements and aims;
second, to. cause them as much trouble as possible while seizing allthe

wealth he can; and third, to report all he learns and to surrender to the
Witch-king most of what he gains through his perfidy and black
rbapic.

The obvious: entrance to the castle is up the road or along the
stream bed, taking care to stay behind the generous cowerat trees amd
shrubbery. At beast two Ore lookouts are on duty. Foo Ube second
floor of Tower 1, where dhe Orcs sleep, and the third foc of the hex-
agonal keep, where a rulned observatory provides a broad view of the
landscape before the gates, Ores are ever-vigilant after dark. During
daylight hours, Ore guards don't see very well and rely instead upem
their keen sense of hearing.

USING ROLEMASTER PROFESSIONS WITH MERP

= AM Fighters, Thieves, and Rangers should be treated as WERP

fessional bonuses (BT-6). AM Clerics, Animists, Healers, and
Astrologers are treated as MRP Amimists.

Prime Stat.

Development Points eiven for “Rogue” (listed later in this
section).

Development Points given for "Warrior Monk” (lisved later in this
section).

— AM Monks are treated as MERP Rangers, but use the Develap-
Men Points given for “Monk” (listed later in this section).

CONVERTING CHARACTERS FROM MERP TO ROLEMASTER

When using material from Aolewanter (A) with MERP, a
Caemaster must keep in mind that while ERP and AM are com-
patiable, RW is more cemplex and detailed than | MERP. This added
Flesdbility in character capabilities will require the GM to make cer-
lain decisions when converting MMERP characters to 2M characters.
The following are suggested guidelines:

— A Warrior may be directly converted to a AM Fighter.
— A Seoul may be directly converted to a AAT Thiel,

— A Ranger may be directly converted ta 0 AM Ranger.
— A Mage may be directly converted to a A Magician.

— A Bard may be directly converted to a AA Bard: however,
Estence will be the M/W Bards realm of power for spells (ie., the
spells must be treated as Essence spells, with the Bard having ac-
com tothe Essence Open Lists NOT the Mentalism Open liscs).

— Af Animist may be directly converted to cither a AM Cleric or a
AAg Animist. The Gel may decide to make this choice based por
the character's nature and use of the MERP spell lists OR be may
allow the player to choose,

AGM may dle other profession conversions for appropriate
characters. For exampe, a Warrior whe concentrated in light armar
ar stealth might beallowed to becomea AW Bogue; a Mage who us-
ed the Ubusions list frequently might be allowed to becomea AM Ilhu-
sonst; an Animist who concentrates in healing might be allowed to
become a AW Healer; etc.

After conversion, character development should proceed using the
RAM character development rues. We supped that a OM allow
MERP characters to keep thelr professional bonuses (ERP Table
BT), buc limit these bonuses boa 10th evel maximum,

Alternatively, a Gamemaster may decxle to allow characters to
continue using MER P character development rules, but convert to
using the eeell liste provided im Spel! Lae,

CONVERTING MERP AND FANTASY HERO STATISTICS

Fantasy Aero DE) from Hero Games is part of the Hero System
family of role Maying products. It uses.a significantly different com-
bot and spell system from those found in ERP and Aolemaster.

Hero, the task should mot present a great obstacle,

TIME

During combat or other situations where game time & precisely
reckoned, Farag Aero uses twelve second “buras” which are di-
vided into one second “segments.” WER and Mefenecter (Fy)
employ ome minute “turns” which are divided inta ten second
“rounds.” For time conversion, we recommend the following:

LMERP Round = 3 FA segments

L WERP Turn > 18 AA segments of 1.2 2H turns

STATS (CHARACTERISTICS)

character's stats (characteristics). FH uses an open- emied system
with most values falling in the 5-20 range. We suggest the following
conversion guideline:

MERP stal = FH stat x4
FH stat = MERP stat / 3 (with a minimum value of 8)

an § value for his Famasy Hero characteristic. The following con-
version table shows how MEARPRA stats and Faafasy Mero stats
rebate.
